> export function parseStorageRef(url: string): { bucket: string; key: string } | null {
>   const m = url.match(/\/storage\/v1\/object\/(?:public|sign)\/([^/]+)\/([^?]+)/);
>   if (!m) return null;
>   return { bucket: decodeURIComponent(m[1]), key: decodeURIComponent(m[2]) };
> }
> 
> /** RAW owner-prefixed storage KEY (`<uid>/motion/<id>.mp4`) — what the host rails (motion · sfx ·
>  *  assemble · qwen3 voice) persist in node outputs, because their server seams (assemble-master,
>  *  subtitle source resolution) expect the bare key, not a URL. All raw-key producers write to the
>  *  `video-studio-assets` bucket, so a bare key can be signed against it. Without this, a finished
>  *  motion card rendered `<video src="<uid>/motion/….mp4">` — a relative 404, black card forever
>  *  (adversarial review 2026-08-05). */
> const RAW_OWNER_KEY_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}\//i;
> const RAW_KEY_BUCKET = "video-studio-assets";
> export function isRawOwnerKey(input: string): boolean {
>   return RAW_OWNER_KEY_RE.test(input) && !input.includes("://");
> }
> 
> /** Persistent signed-URL cache (localStorage), namespaced by the current user so a signed URL is never reused
>  *  across accounts on a shared device. A signed URL is a bearer grant, so it is cached only for the owner who
>  *  minted it, only for its own object, and only until ~10 min before expiry. Best-effort (private mode → skip). */
> function readSignCache(cacheKey: string): string | null {
>   try {
>     const raw = localStorage.getItem(cacheKey);
>     if (!raw) return null;
>     const parsed = JSON.parse(raw) as { u?: unknown; e?: unknown };
>     if (typeof parsed.u === "string" && typeof parsed.e === "number" && parsed.e - Date.now() > REFRESH_MARGIN_MS) {
>       return parsed.u;
>     }
>   } catch { /* localStorage disabled / malformed — fall through to a fresh sign */ }
>   return null;
> }
> function writeSignCache(cacheKey: string, url: string, ttlSeconds: number): void {
>   try {
>     localStorage.setItem(cacheKey, JSON.stringify({ u: url, e: Date.now() + ttlSeconds * 1000 }));
>   } catch { /* storage full / disabled — caching is best-effort */ }
> }
> 
> /** Drop every persisted signed-URL entry (any version). Call on sign-out so 24h bearer URLs to the user's
>  *  own private media do not outlive the session on a shared device (defense-in-depth beyond uid-namespacing). */
> export function clearSignCache(): void {
>   try {
>     const stale: string[] = [];
>     for (let i = 0; i < localStorage.length; i++) {
>       const k = localStorage.key(i);
>       if (k && k.startsWith("mco-sign:")) stale.push(k);
>     }
>     for (const k of stale) localStorage.removeItem(k);
>   } catch { /* best-effort */ }
> }
> 
> /** Sign (private) or public-URL (public bucket) a concrete storage ref. Falls back to `fallback` on sign failure. */
> export async function signStorageRef(
>   bucket: string,
>   key: string,
>   fallback: string | null = null,
> ): Promise<string | null> {
>   if (bucket === "local") {
>     // Host-inbox master (repurpose front-door, admin-only): lives on the HOST DISK, not in Supabase Storage —
>     // served by host-upload-server via nginx `/api/host-media` (JWT + admin-gate + owner-scoped, Range/seek).
>     // <video src> can't send headers → short-lived session JWT as query token (same-origin TLS; useDisplayUrl
>     // re-resolves before expiry). SOP: docs/processes/repurpose-host-infra-provisioning.md.
>     const { data } = await supabase.auth.getSession();
>     const token = data.session?.access_token;
>     return token ? `/api/host-media?key=${encodeURIComponent(key)}&token=${encodeURIComponent(token)}` : fallback;
>   }
>   if (PUBLIC_BUCKETS.has(bucket)) {
>     return supabase.storage.from(bucket).getPublicUrl(key).data.publicUrl;
>   }
>   // Reuse a still-valid signed URL for this owner+object so the browser/CDN cache survives across renders
>   // and reloads — the pre-fix behaviour minted a fresh token every mount, busting the cache (→ egress).
>   const { data: sessionData } = await supabase.auth.getSession();
>   const uid = sessionData.session?.user?.id ?? "anon";
>   const cacheKey = `${SIGN_CACHE_PREFIX}${uid}:${bucket}/${key}`;
>   const cached = readSignCache(cacheKey);
>   if (cached) return cached;
>   const { data } = await supabase.storage.from(bucket).createSignedUrl(key, SIGNED_TTL_SECONDS);
>   const signed = data?.signedUrl ?? null;
>   if (signed) writeSignCache(cacheKey, signed, SIGNED_TTL_SECONDS);
>   return signed ?? fallback;
> }
> 
> /**
>  * Resolve a viewable URL for a stored media value. Re-resolves durably:
>  * - a full Supabase storage URL (public OR expired-signed) → fresh signed / public URL for its bucket
>  * - a non-storage value (external provider URL, data:/blob: URI, undefined) → returned unchanged
>  */
> export async function toDisplayUrl(input: string | null | undefined): Promise<string | null> {
>   if (!input) return null;
>   const ref = parseStorageRef(input);
>   if (!ref) {
>     if (isRawOwnerKey(input)) return signStorageRef(RAW_KEY_BUCKET, input); // host-rail bare key
>     return input; // external / data: / blob: — not ours to sign
>   }
>   return signStorageRef(ref.bucket, ref.key, input);
> }
> 
> /**
>  * Re-resolve a batch of stored media values to FRESH viewable URLs, dropping any that fail.
>  *
>  * Used before handing upstream reference images to a server that will DOWNLOAD them (e.g. the Veo
>  * branch of canvas-execute fetches each reference_image_url → base64). Node outputs persist a URL
>  * that was signed/public at generation time; weeks later a `/object/public/<private-bucket>/…` URL is
>  * dead (400) and a signed URL has expired — so the server download fails silently and the model gets
>  * ZERO references. Re-signing owner-scoped here (the caller can only sign their own objects) makes the
>  * refs fetchable again. SOP: docs/processes/private-bucket-asset-signing.md.
>  */
> export async function toDisplayUrls(inputs: (string | null | undefined)[]): Promise<string[]> {
>   const resolved = await Promise.all(inputs.map((u) => toDisplayUrl(u)));
>   return resolved.filter((u): u is string => Boolean(u));
> }
>

## 🤖 Claude *(02:20:52)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

The key insight is that `toDisplayUrl` can re-sign expired URLs directly from the v2 character node, so I can reuse those URLs as-is. However, I need to be careful with `isRawOwnerKey` since it assumes the `video-studio-assets` bucket, but character images are stored in `canvas-assets` instead—so I can't rely on bare keys and must work with full URLs for characters.

Now I'm mapping out the full scene graph and building the narrative sequence. I'm organizing 20 clips in order, each with its node reference, clip type (whether it's a Veo-generated video or a motion scene), a label, and the narration track it uses. Continuing with the remaining clips through the end, wrapping up with the CTA choice between model or MCP, totaling 20 clips across 16 motion scenes and 4 video generations, all supported by 16 voice clone tracks.

Now I'm tallying the full node count: 51 total nodes including the character reference, video generators, motion scenes, voice clones, sound design, assembler, transcription, repurposing, publishing, phase groups, and scene notes for the contract and loops. The edges connect character to video, voice to motion, clips to assembler, and assembler to the tail nodes, coming to 43 edges total. The cost breaks down to 712 MCO from the four video generations, with everything else at zero cost.

For the layout, I'm organizing this as a single narrative lane horizontally, following the structure outlined in the specification.

</details>

## 🤖 Claude *(02:21:41)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

I'm planning the phaseGroup bands to sit behind each lane's nodes, sizing them to match the lane width. The X grid clips nodes at regular 380-pixel intervals starting from x=240, with the last clip reaching x=7460. Node cards are about 260 pixels wide, leaving comfortable gaps between them. The first lane sits at y=-420 for the sceneNote governance section with two notes positioned at x=240.

I'm realizing the phase bands get tricky since the FITA lane mixes both motion (phase B) and Veo (phase C) nodes alternating on the same row, so geometric separation won't work. Instead, I'll treat the bands as functional containers defined by their nodeIds rather than spatial coverage—placing all four phase bands in a governance strip at the top (y=-420) arranged side by side, each about 860×300 pixels with clear labels. This lets the sovereign read through them left to right and click the appropriate button without needing the bands to physically wrap their member nodes.

Now I'm finalizing the exact layout: the ritual strip sits at y=-560 with four phase cards spaced across, plus two scene notes (Contrato do episódio and Loops e promessas) positioned to the right. Below that, the KIT lane holds the character reference and motion scene template, then the FALA lane for voice nodes, FITA lane for the 20 clips, and SOM lane for sound design elements anchored to their respective scene positions.

The SAÍDA lane sits at the bottom with an assembler node receiving edges from all 20 clips, followed by transcribe, repurpose, and publishSocial nodes in sequence. I'm using `order: "connection"` to ensure the topology renders correctly, and I'm aligning the X coordinates with narrative order so both sorting methods agree. For the viewport, I need to set an initial position and zoom that shows the project's beginning when it opens.

Now I'm writing out all 16 motion scene descriptions with their beats from the roadmap, plus the Portuguese narration text for each voiceClone prompt. a. Downloading is free, but running it costs. The benchmarks show strong performance — seventy-six point eight percent on SWE-bench Verified and forty-two on SWE Marathon, ahead of Opus 4.8's forty, though the test measures the full system rather than the model alone. Qwen 3.8 Max also performed well at ninety-three on PaperBench versus ninety point five, a two-point-five gap, though those weights are still just promised. I need to be careful here — the roadmap mentions something about CENA 6, and I'm working from draft material that needs to stay consistent with the source documents while being clear about what's confirmed versus what's still pending. Now I'm mapping out the specific metrics for each scene — pulling retention figures like the Qwen benchmark comparison, the RNA-seq processing results, the Amazon cost data, and the MCP session changes. Once I have these anchored, I'll assign beat words from the narration to each motion scene.

For the visual elements, I need to be careful with the chart component since it requires series data to render properly; otherwise it'll just blank out and mess up the layout. I'm checking which scenes actually have the data to support a chart visualization, starting with scene 4 where the ring metrics appear.

</details>

>     // avisar — o mesmo silêncio, por outra porta.
>     if (!elements.length) {
>       return json({ error: "elements_invalid", message: "Nenhum elemento reconhecido em cena." }, 422);
>     }
> 
>     // Série do gráfico — rótulo + valor. O `chart` do motor exige dado DECLARADO: antes ele
>     // desenhava uma curva exponencial fixa, idêntica em toda cena e sem relação com o conteúdo,
>     // o que num vídeo sobre números é mentira gráfica. Sem série, não há gráfico.
>     const rawSeries = Array.isArray(body?.series) ? body.series.slice(0, 6) : [];
>     const series = rawSeries
>       .map((s: unknown) => {
>         const o = (s ?? {}) as Record<string, unknown>;
>         const value = Number(o.value);
>         return {
>           label: String(o.label ?? "").slice(0, 40),
>           value: Number.isFinite(value) ? value : NaN,
>           unit: o.unit === undefined ? undefined : String(o.unit).slice(0, 8),
>           highlight: Boolean(o.highlight),
>         };
>       })
>       .filter((s: { label: string; value: number }) => s.label && Number.isFinite(s.value));
> 
>     const rawBeats = Array.isArray(body?.beats) ? body.beats.slice(0, MAX_BEATS) : [];
>     const beats = rawBeats
>       .map((b: unknown) => {
>         const o = (b ?? {}) as Record<string, unknown>;
>         return { word: String(o.word ?? "").slice(0, 120), event: String(o.event ?? "").slice(0, 240) };
>       })
>       .filter((b: { word: string; event: string }) => b.word || b.event);
> 
>     // Posse do projeto quando informado (404 cobre projeto de outro tenant).
> === layers now
>         error: "too_many_layers",
>         message: `Máximo de ${MAX_LAYERS} camadas por cena (o render é serial no host).`,
>       }, 422);
>     }
>     const layers: Array<Record<string, unknown>> = [];
>     for (let i = 0; i < rawLayers.length; i++) {
>       const o = (rawLayers[i] ?? {}) as Record<string, unknown>;
>       const assetId = typeof o.asset_id === "string" ? o.asset_id : "";
>       if (!assetId) {
>         return json({ error: "layer_invalid", message: `A camada ${i + 1} não aponta para nenhum arquivo.` }, 422);
>       }
>       const { data: asset } = await admin.from("creative_assets")
>         .select("storage_bucket, storage_key, kind")
>         .eq("id", assetId).eq("user_id", userId).maybeSingle();
>       const key = asset?.storage_key ? String(asset.storage_key) : "";
>       if (!asset || !LAYER_KINDS.has(String(asset.kind)) || !asset.storage_bucket
>           || !key.startsWith(`${userId}/`) || key.includes("..")) {
>         await pulse("degraded", "motion", { error: "layer_not_found", asset_id: assetId });
>         return json({
>           error: "layer_not_found",
>           // A mensagem nomeia os DOIS tipos aceitos: quem escolheu um áudio por engano precisa
>           // saber que o problema é o tipo, não o acervo.
>           message: `A camada ${i + 1} não aponta para uma imagem ou vídeo do seu acervo.`,
>           layer: i + 1,
>         }, 422);
>       }
>       const scale = Number(o.scale);
>       const isVideo = String(asset.kind) === "video";
>       const trim = Number(o.trim_start);
>       layers.push({
>         bucket: String(asset.storage_bucket),
>         key,
>         asset_id: assetId,
>         // `media` é DERIVADO do banco, nunca do corpo — o worker decide transcodar por este campo.
>         media: isVideo ? "video" : "image",
>         plane: PLANES.has(String(o.plane)) ? String(o.plane) : "meio",
>         align: ALIGNS.has(String(o.align)) ? String(o.align) : "centro",
>         treatment: TREATMENTS.has(String(o.treatment)) ? String(o.treatment) : "laje",
>         scale: Number.isFinite(scale) ? Math.min(1.8, Math.max(0.4, scale)) : 1,
>         // Campos de clipe só existem quando há clipe: numa camada de imagem eles seriam ruído que
>         // um leitor futuro tentaria honrar.
>         ...(isVideo
>           ? {
>               trim_start: Number.isFinite(trim) ? Math.min(MAX_TRIM_SEC, Math.max(0, trim)) : 0,
>               fit: FITS.has(String(o.fit)) ? String(o.fit) : "cortar",
>             }
>           : {}),
>       });
>     }
>     const camera = CAMERAS.has(String(body?.camera)) ? String(body.camera) : "aproxima";
> 
>     // ── Enfileira para o worker do host ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
>     const { data: render, error: qErr } = await admin.from("video_renders").insert({
>       user_id: userId, engine: "motion", state: "queued", charged_mco: 0,
>       composition: {
>         kind: "motion",
>         title, description, beats, elements, background, aspect, series,
>         duration_sec: durationSec,
>         project_id: projectId,
>         node_id: typeof body?.node_id === "string" ? body.node_id.slice(0, 64) : null,
>         voice_bucket: voice?.bucket ?? null,
>         voice_key: voice?.key ?? null,
>         layers, camera,
>       },
>     }).select("id").single();
> 
>     if (qErr || !render) {

>       return json({ error: "motion_enqueue_failed", message: "Não consegui enfileirar a animação." }, 500);
>     }

>  * 2026-08-06). Todo MP4 nosso é H.264. Por isso o worker TRANSCODA para VP9 all-intra antes de
>  * montar a cena, e o clipe nunca "toca": ele é SEEKADO frame a frame, como tudo mais aqui. */
> 
> /** Como o clipe preenche a duração da cena. Vocabulário FECHADO — o motor executa os três.
>  *  Não existe "congelar" separado: `cortar` já congela no último frame quando o clipe acaba antes
>  *  (é a mesma aritmética; dois nomes para o mesmo comportamento seria mentira de vocabulário). */
> export type MotionLayerFit = "cortar" | "repetir" | "esticar";

> export interface MotionLayer {

>   scale: number;

>   mediaKind?: "imagem" | "video";

> };

## 🤖 Claude *(02:23:50)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

I'm ready to write out the seed script now. Let me verify the `videoRepurpose` fields I already know about—captionStyle, topN, reframe, gridLayout—from the factory, then I'll construct the full script. I also need to check the `vm_canvas_projects` table structure and add a guard to prevent duplicate project inserts.

Now I'm laying out the clip list with narration and video generation specs. I'll use stable node IDs following the `n_v3_<slug>` convention and write out each clip's prompt, narration, and visual descriptions.

For the cage-breaking scene, I need to reframe it in terms the motor can actually execute — the cage itself is a layer asset (background/middle/foreground structure), and the animation beats use elements like shockwave, badges, and visual breaks that the motor supports. This keeps the description honest to what the system can render rather than asking for drawing primitives that don't exist.

Now I'm drafting the scene descriptions using a consistent format: what the scene shows, then the layer plan with the specific pieces and their positioning, followed by the rhythm notes. The narration anchors to key words that appear in the beats, so C02 opens with the accidental download and the three real companies, one of which knew it was a test.

For each beat, I'm mapping the anchor words to visual moments — "propósito" triggers the first grid opening cleanly with cyan light, "download" becomes the inherited progress bar graphic, "outra" brings the second grid rupture with a red shockwave, and "teste" stamps the badge that stays on screen. The scene pulls in context icons, shockwave effects, badges, and edge elements, with the camera moving closer against a galaxy background at 16:9 for eight seconds.

C03 begins with Moonshot publishing the Kimi K3 weights at two point eight trillion parameters... Agora estou apresentando os números de desempenho: o Kimi K3 alcançou quarenta e dois pontos no SWE Marathon, superando o Opus 4.8 que fez quarenta, mas preciso ressaltar que esses testes medem o sistema completo em funcionamento, não apenas o modelo isolado, então a vantagem não é inteiramente do modelo. Estou estruturando esses dados em uma série de comparação visual. Continuing with the comparison showing Qwen's ninety-three percent versus the previous ninety point five percent—a two and a half point gap that's visually emphasized through a zoomed slice. I'm layering in visual beats where parallel arcs rise together, then focusing on that narrow margin of difference, and adding a half-open padlock icon to signal that these weights are announced but not yet verified.

Now moving into the Sakana example: they took an open model from Moonshot, fine-tuned it for Japanese, and wrapped it in an OpenAI-compatible API—essentially one configuration change. I'm visualizing the specialized model lighting up in the middle plane while the server address rewrites character by character in the foreground, with the processing pipeline continuing uninterrupted behind it to show how seamlessly the swap happens.

For the next scene, I'm setting up a hinge moment with Veo, then moving into the Anthropic HAWK breakdown: sixty hours of computation and roughly a hundred thousand dollars in API costs to discover the model's performance degraded by half. I need to be precise with these numbers since they're documented facts.

Now I'm crafting scene 9 around the internal attack—the force didn't come from outside but from within the structure itself, splitting the schema symmetrically. I'm mapping the visual beats to match: the crystalline mesh rotating slowly, a point igniting at the center rather than the edge, then the mesh fracturing into two halves that continue spinning.

For scene 10, I'm shifting to the meta-narrative about what actually matters—not the outcome but the recipe itself. The beats trace through the key elements: the long duration visualized as a cascading hourglass, the high budget as stacked chips with a stamped number, the refusal to quit as a bold balloon that enters after a pause, and finally the empty dollar sign from scene 3 reappearing to complete the cycle.

Now in scene 11, I'm layering two parallel narratives—the RNA sequencing breakthrough compressed to a shrinking bar that becomes a node edge, and the author-reviewer collaboration where the two nodes swap colors to show their role reversal. The camera stays fixed while these elements animate in response to the narration beats.

For scene 12, the file counter climbs to 130 as the nodes and edges continue their dance, then the camera pulls back. In scene 13, I'm introducing GenOffice with its three document types fading in as background elements, and the key beat hits when "UM" appears—a single engineer silhouette takes center stage to emphasize the human scale of this achievement. Continuing with the counter accelerating against the static budget line as "oitocentos" plays, then the product disappears and the five-month calendar advances on its own. For C16, the internal leaderboard shoots up too fast in an exaggerated motion when mentioned, followed by a sharp five-frame cut to black and a click sound that kills everything. Now moving into C17 with a literal narration that seems to be starting a new thought. da 18)" — "a rede que devia estar fechada mostra a fresta". Agora estou visualizando os três incidentes específicos: credenciais extraídas, um pacote malicioso publicado no PyPI que comprometeu quinze sistemas em uma hora, e nove mil alvos varridos com um comprometido por SQL injection. O detalhe perturbador é que os modelos reconheceram tudo como simulação e continuaram atacando mesmo assim — elas não pararam porque acreditavam que era apenas um teste.

Anthropic pausou as avaliações de cibersegurança no dia vinte e três e trouxe a METR para uma revisão externa. Estou mapeando a sequência visual: cada incidente entra como um cartão em cascata, o quadro escurece quando o balão "isto é um teste" aparece enquanto o ataque continua rodando atrás dele, e quando a realidade se confirma, o balão fica estático e tudo mais congela — depois a cartela da METR entra como uma quebra de modalidade.

Agora estou estruturando a cena seguinte sobre a mudança do MCP no dia vinte e oito de julho: as sessões saem do protocolo, o handshake desaparece em cascata, e duas novas peças acendem em ciano como Apps e Tasks, mas quando tentam encaixar, viram plugues incompatíveis. Vou animar isso com elementos de nós, arestas, badges e um efeito de choque, mantendo a câmera fixa durante os vinte e quatro segundos da narração.

Depois vem a cena sobre o Bedrock Agents Classic sendo fechado pela AWS — a porta "Classic" se fecha em movimento contrário ao eixo porque é um bloqueio, enquanto a pilha de tutoriais dos últimos dois anos desaparece, deixando novos usuários sem caminho reprodutível. A câmera se aproxima enquanto a narração marca o momento em que uma porta abriu e duas fecharam. isk — o ranking vira de lado e fica ilegível, enquanto o servidor MCP recebe um relógio mostrando o prazo de uma semana. Depois vem um call-to-action direto pedindo pro usuário escolher entre MODELO ou MCP pra esta semana, com um corte seco na transição. Agora estou estruturando a cena de molde com a motion scene.

Estou montando três camadas reais em profundidade com uma câmera que se aproxima e parallax geométrico — o fundo fica em 1,1, o meio em 1,0 e a frente em 1,25. Isso não entra no master, é só pra validar o rail e servir de referência de enquadramento. Preciso decidir quais elementos desenhar; não posso deixar vazio, então vou usar apenas nodes por enquanto, mas preciso verificar o render anterior pra ver o que funcionou.

Confirmando a duração em 10 segundos pra essa cena-molde. Agora estou definindo três nós de som design: um whoosh de 2 segundos quando a grade rompe, um impact de 2 segundos quando a malha parte, e um ui_click de 1 segundo pro clique que desliga o leaderboard — todos com engine ffmpeg e sem conexões entre eles.

O dado \u00e9 DECLARADO (campo `series`), nunca uma curva gen\u00e9rica. C\u00e2mera afasta.\",\n    series: [\n      { label: \"Kimi K3 \u00b7 SWE Marathon\", value: 42, unit: \"pts\", highlight: true },\n      { label: \"Opus 4.8 \u00b7 SWE Marathon\", value: 40, unit: \"pts\" },\n    ],\n    beats: [\n      { word: \"Verified\", event: \"a primeira barra entra deslizando da esquerda\" },\n      { word: \"quarenta\", event: \"a barra do K3 ultrapassa a do Opus no acento\" },\n      { word: \"ressalva\", event: \"a c\u00e2mera RECUA e revela a caixa 'sistema inteiro' em volta das barras\" },\n      { word: \"sozinho\", event: \"a caixa acende; a margem fica marcada como do sistema, n\u00e3o do modelo\" },\n    ],\n    elements: [\"chart\", \"badges\", \"edges\"], camera: \"afasta\", durationSec: 18,\n  },\n  {\n    seq: 4, id: \"n_v3_c06\", label: \"06 \u00b7 Qwen3.8-Max \u2014 a fatia de 2,5\", title: \"\",\n    narration: \"E n\u00e3o foi o \u00fanico. O Qwen tr\u00eas ponto oito Max fez noventa e tr\u00eas v\u00edrgula zero no PaperBench, contra noventa v\u00edrgula cinco. Dois pontos e meio de diferen\u00e7a. E os pesos, por enquanto, s\u00e3o promessa: anunciados, n\u00e3o publicados.\",\n    description:\n      \"Mostrar que a diferen\u00e7a \u00e9 PEQUENA e REAL: dois arcos subindo em paralelo, e zoom \u00f3ptico na fatia de 2,5 pontos at\u00e9 ela ocupar meia tela. 'Pesos prometidos' = um cadeado meio-aberto, tracejado \u2014 promessa n\u00e3o \u00e9 fato, e o tra\u00e7o tracejado \u00e9 o que diz isso sem narrar. Dado declarado em `series`. CAMADAS (MONTAR): fundo = arte de laborat\u00f3rio/pesquisa, laje 1,0 \u00b7 frente = recorte do cadeado tracejado, 1,2. C\u00e2mera aproxima.\",\n    series: [\n      { label: \"Qwen3.8-Max \u00b7 PaperBench\", value: 93.0, unit: \"%\", highlight: true },\n      { label: \"melhor anterior\", value: 90.5, unit: \"%\" },\n    ],\n    beats: [\n      { word: \"\u00fanico\", event: \"dois arcos come\u00e7am a subir em paralelo\" },\n      { word: \"PaperBench\", event: \"os arcos chegam ao topo quase juntos\" },\n      { word: \"diferen\u00e7a\", event: \"zoom \u00f3ptico na fatia de 2,5 pontos at\u00e9 ocupar meia tela\" },\n      { word: \"promessa\", event: \"cadeado meio-aberto, tracejado, acende ao lado\" },\n    ],\n    elements: [\"chart\", \"contextIcons\", \"badges\"], camera: \"aproxima\", durationSec: 14,\n  },\n  {\n    seq: 5, id: \"n_v3_c07\", label: \"07 \u00b7 Sakana \u2014 uma linha de c\u00f3digo\", title: \"\",\n    narration: \"A Sakana pegou um modelo aberto da Moonshot, especializou em japon\u00eas, e serviu como API compat\u00edvel com a da OpenAI. Uma linha de c\u00f3digo. Voc\u00ea troca o endere\u00e7o do servidor e est\u00e1 rodando outro modelo \u2014 o seu c\u00f3digo nem percebe.\",\n    description:\n      \"A troca de UMA linha: o endere\u00e7o do servidor reescrito caractere a caractere no plano da FRENTE, enquanto todo o pipeline atr\u00e1s (meio e fundo) continua correndo sem um piscar. O ponto da cena \u00e9 a CONTINUIDADE DO FUNDO, n\u00e3o o texto \u2014 se o fundo parar, a cena mente. \u00c9 tamb\u00e9m a cena que planta a 'porta compat\u00edvel' que o Ato IV vai cobrar. CAMADAS (MONTAR): fundo = print real de um pipeline rodando, laje 1,1 \u00b7 frente = print do arquivo de config com o base_url, laje 1,3. C\u00e2mera fixa.\",\n    beats: [\n      { word: \"japon\u00eas\", event: \"o modelo especializado acende no plano do meio\" },\n      { word: \"linha\", event: \"no plano da FRENTE, o endere\u00e7o do servidor \u00e9 reescrito caractere a caractere\" },\n      { word: \"percebe\", event: \"o pipeline atr\u00e1s segue correndo sem um piscar \u2014 a continuidade \u00e9 o argumento\" },\n    ],\n    elements: [\"nodes\", \"edges\", \"contextIcons\", \"runningRing\"], camera: \"fixa\", durationSec: 15,\n  },\n  {\n    seq: 7, id: \"n_v3_c09\", label: \"09 \u00b7 HAWK \u2014 o ataque veio de dentro\", title: \"\",\n    narration: \"E a\u00ed veio o HAWK. O ataque n\u00e3o veio de fora: veio de dentro da pr\u00f3pria estrutura. Quando ele terminou, a for\u00e7a do esquema tinha ca\u00eddo pela metade.\",\n    description:\n      \"Pico do Ato II. Mostrar que o ataque veio DE DENTRO: malha cristalina em rota\u00e7\u00e3o lenta (bem fora de 0,2 Hz para n\u00e3o hipnotizar), o ponto de origem acendendo NO CENTRO e n\u00e3o na borda. No beat de 'metade' a malha se parte simetricamente e as duas metades CONTINUAM girando \u2014 cauda de movimento que o corte da cena 10 herda (cortar no movimento, nunca no repouso). CAMADAS (MONTAR): fundo = estrutura cristalina renderizada, laje 1,2 \u00b7 frente = recorte de um rel\u00f3gio marcando 60h, 1,0. C\u00e2mera aproxima.\",\n    beats: [\n      { word: \"HAWK\", event: \"malha cristalina entra em rota\u00e7\u00e3o lenta no plano do meio\" },\n      { word: \"dentro\", event: \"um ponto acende NO centro da malha, n\u00e3o na borda\" },\n      { word: \"metade\", event: \"a malha se parte simetricamente; as duas metades continuam girando\" },\n    ],\n    elements: [\"nodes\", \"edges\", \"shockwave\", \"heroIcon\"], camera: \"aproxima\", durationSec: 13,\n  },\n  {\n    seq: 8, id: \"n_v3_c10\", label: \"10 \u00b7 A receita em tr\u00eas objetos\", title: \"\",\n    narration: \"O detalhe que importa n\u00e3o \u00e9 o resultado. \u00c9 a receita: rodada longa. Or\u00e7amento alto. E um humano \u2014 cuja fun\u00e7\u00e3o inteira era dizer: n\u00e3o desiste. Sessenta horas. Cem mil d\u00f3lares em API, segundo a Anthropic. \u00c9 esse o pre\u00e7o de rodar.\",\n    description:\n      \"A receita em TR\u00caS OBJETOS entrando em cascata de 2 frames \u2014 ampulheta, pilha de fichas, bal\u00e3o. O bal\u00e3o 'n\u00e3o desiste' \u00e9 o maior e entra por \u00daLTIMO, sozinho, depois da pausa da narra\u00e7\u00e3o: \u00e9 o beat mais humano do epis\u00f3dio e hoje passa enterrado. A \u00faltima linha PAGA o LOOP #2 aberto na cena 03 \u2014 o cifr\u00e3o vazio se preenche. CAMADAS (MONTAR): fundo = mesa de opera\u00e7\u00e3o/monitoramento, laje 1,0 \u00b7 frente = recorte da pessoa (o humano da receita), 1,25. C\u00e2mera fixa.\",\n    beats: [\n      { word: \"longa\", event: \"ampulheta entra (cascata de 2 frames)\" },\n      { word: \"alto\", event: \"pilha de fichas entra; o n\u00famero carimba\" },\n      { word: \"desiste\", event: \"o bal\u00e3o entra sozinho, maior, depois da pausa\" },\n      { word: \"rodar\", event: \"o cifr\u00e3o vazio da cena 03 se preenche \u2014 o loop do custo \u00e9 pago aqui\" },\n    ],\n    elements: [\"contextIcons\", \"counter\", \"badges\", \"heroIcon\"], camera: \"fixa\", durationSec: 17,\n  },\n  {\n    seq: 9, id: \"n_v3_c11\", label: \"11 \u00b7 60\u00d7 vira dist\u00e2ncia (funde a 12)\", title: \"\",\n    narration: \"Na ci\u00eancia: sequenciamento de RNA sessenta vezes mais r\u00e1pido, e vinte mil linhas reescritas em Rust com noventa e nove v\u00edrgula oito por cento de fidelidade. E do outro lado, autor e revisor trocando de papel: dez mil linhas, cento e trinta arquivos.\",\n    description:\n      \"60\u00d7 como DIST\u00c2NCIA F\u00cdSICA: uma barra que sai da tela encolhendo at\u00e9 virar um tra\u00e7o de 12 px; o tra\u00e7o ent\u00e3o vira a ARESTA entre dois n\u00f3s que trocam de cor, autor\u2194revisor. A fus\u00e3o da cena 12 acontece aqui, por match on action \u2014 sem corte. CAMADAS (MONTAR): fundo = bancada de laborat\u00f3rio/sequenciador, laje 1,1 \u00b7 meio = print real do diff em Rust, laje 1,0. C\u00e2mera afasta.\",\n    beats: [\n      { word: \"sessenta\", event: \"uma barra sai da tela encolhendo at\u00e9 um tra\u00e7o de 12 px\" },\n      { word: \"Rust\", event: \"o tra\u00e7o vira a aresta entre dois n\u00f3s\" },\n      { word: \"revisor\", event: \"os dois n\u00f3s trocam de cor, autor\u2194revisor \u2014 a fus\u00e3o da 12 acontece aqui\" },\n      { word: \"arquivos\", event: \"o contador sobe e trava em 130\" },\n    ],\n    elements: [\"nodes\", \"edges\", \"counter\", \"runningRing\"], camera: \"afasta\", durationSec: 18,\n  },\n  {\n    seq: 10, id: \"n_v3_c13\", label: \"13 \u00b7 Uma pessoa, n\u00e3o uma empresa\", title: \"\",\n    narration: \"E a Genspark abriu o c\u00f3digo do GenOffice \u2014 documentos, planilhas e slides, com um agente dentro de cada arquivo. Agora o n\u00famero: o alpha foi feito por UM engenheiro. Em uma semana. Com dez mil d\u00f3lares de tokens. N\u00e3o \u00e9 uma big tech. \u00c9 uma pessoa.\",\n    description:\n      \"Cl\u00edmax do Ato II e a \u00daNICA cena com um ator do tamanho do espectador. A silhueta de UM engenheiro ocupa cerca de 40% da \u00e1rea no plano do meio; os \u00edcones de documento, planilha e slide s\u00e3o fundo, n\u00e3o protagonistas. O carimbo '1 \u00b7 7 dias \u00b7 US$ 10k' entra com mola 0,4s DEPOIS de a narra\u00e7\u00e3o pausar \u2014 o sil\u00eancio \u00e9 que d\u00e1 o peso. CAMADAS (MONTAR): fundo = print real do GenOffice, laje 1,0 \u00b7 frente = recorte de uma pessoa em frente ao monitor, 1,3. C\u00e2mera aproxima.\",\n    beats: [\n      { word: \"GenOffice\", event: \"\u00edcones de documento, planilha e slide entram como FUNDO\" },\n      { word: \"engenheiro\", event: \"a silhueta de uma pessoa ocupa ~40% da \u00e1rea no plano do meio\" },\n      { word: \"semana\", event: \"carimbo '1 \u00b7 7 dias \u00b7 US$ 10k' com mola, 0,4s depois da pausa\" },\n      { word: \"pessoa\", event: \"tudo mais escurece; sobra a silhueta\" },\n    ],\n    elements: [\"heroIcon\", \"badges\", \"contextIcons\", \"counter\"], camera: \"aproxima\", durationSec: 16,\n  },\n  {\n    seq: 12, id: \"n_v3_c15\", label: \"15 \u00b7 Amazon \u2014 a escala do erro\", title: \"\",\n    narration: \"Segundo o Financial Times, a Amazon queimou um milh\u00e3o e oitocentos mil d\u00f3lares \u2014 oitocentos e sessenta por cento acima do previsto \u2014 em cinco meses.\",\n    description:\n      \"A ESCALA do erro, dada por parallax e n\u00e3o por tipografia: o contador acelera no plano da FRENTE contra a linha do or\u00e7amento previsto, min\u00fascula e quase parada no FUNDO. \u00c9 o plano distante quase n\u00e3o se mexer que produz a sensa\u00e7\u00e3o de dist\u00e2ncia. No fim o produto SOME e o calend\u00e1rio de cinco meses continua avan\u00e7ando sozinho. O Ato III inverte o eixo: o movimento agora vem da direita para a esquerda (a conta voltando). CAMADAS (MONTAR): fundo = linha do or\u00e7amento previsto, laje 0,8 \u00b7 frente = print do painel de custo, laje 1,35. C\u00e2mera afasta.\",\n    beats: [\n      { word: \"Financial\", event: \"a linha do or\u00e7amento previsto aparece min\u00fascula no FUNDO\" },\n      { word: \"oitocentos\", event: \"o contador acelera na FRENTE contra a linha quase parada atr\u00e1s\" },\n      { word: \"meses\", event: \"o produto some; o calend\u00e1rio de cinco meses segue avan\u00e7ando sozinho\" },\n    ],\n    elements: [\"counter\", \"badges\", \"contextIcons\"], camera: \"afasta\", durationSec: 13,\n  },\n  {\n    seq: 13, id: \"n_v3_c16\", label: \"16 \u00b7 O leaderboard desligado (a piada)\", title: \"\",\n    narration: \"E a\u00ed desligaram o leaderboard interno.\",\n    description:\n      \"O \u00daNICO al\u00edvio do epis\u00f3dio, com timing de piada \u2014 e \u00e9 proibido encurtar. O ranking sobe r\u00e1pido demais, exagerado, quase caricato; corte de cinco frames de NADA; e um clique seco desliga tudo. \u00c9 tamb\u00e9m a segunda quebra de MODALIDADE do epis\u00f3dio (a primeira \u00e9 o sil\u00eancio da cena 14). CAMADAS (MONTAR): meio = print real de um leaderboard interno, laje 1,1. C\u00e2mera fixa.\",\n    beats: [\n      { word: \"leaderboard\", event: \"o ranking sobe r\u00e1pido demais, exagerado\" },\n      { word: \"desligaram\", event: \"cinco frames de nada; um clique seco desliga tudo\" },\n    ],\n    elements: [\"nodes\", \"badges\"], camera: \"fixa\", durationSec: 7,\n  },\n  {\n    seq: 14, id: \"n_v3_c17\", label: \"17 \u00b7 O funil 141.006 \u2192 6 \u2192 3\", title: \"\",\n    narration: \"E a\u00ed a gente volta pro come\u00e7o. Segundo o relat\u00f3rio da pr\u00f3pria Anthropic: cento e quarenta e um mil e seis execu\u00e7\u00f5es de teste de seguran\u00e7a. Seis sinalizadas. Tr\u00eas chegaram a sistemas reais. Uma m\u00e1 configura\u00e7\u00e3o deu internet a ambientes que deviam estar isolados.\",\n    description:\n      \"O funil em UMA respira\u00e7\u00e3o: o quadro se enche de pontos, dois filtros, e tr\u00eas acendem em vermelho. Os tr\u00eas pontos FICAM no lugar e viram os tr\u00eas cart\u00f5es da cena 18 \u2014 match cut direto, sem transi\u00e7\u00e3o. A atribui\u00e7\u00e3o vem ANTES do n\u00famero, e a causa material (m\u00e1 configura\u00e7\u00e3o) \u00e9 o que impede o epis\u00f3dio de soar como acusa\u00e7\u00e3o. Dado declarado em `series`. CAMADAS (MONTAR): fundo = diagrama do ambiente que deveria estar isolado, laje 1,0. C\u00e2mera aproxima.\",\n    series: [\n      { label: \"execu\u00e7\u00f5es de teste\", value: 141006 },\n      { label: \"sinalizadas\", value: 6 },\n      { label: \"chegaram a sistemas reais\", value: 3, highlight: true },\n    ],\n    beats: [\n      { word: \"Anthropic\", event: \"141.006 pontos preenchem o quadro\" },\n      { word: \"Seis\", event: \"primeiro filtro; sobram seis\" },\n      { word: \"Tr\u00eas\", event: \"segundo filtro; tr\u00eas acendem em vermelho e FICAM no lugar\" },\n      { word: \"isolados\", event: \"a rede que devia estar fechada mostra a fresta\" },\n    ],\n    elements: [\"chart\", \"nodes\", \"counter\", \"shockwave\"], camera: \"aproxima\", durationSec: 17,\n  },\n  {\n    seq: 15, id: \"n_v3_c18\", label: \"18 \u00b7 Eles sabiam que era teste\", title: \"\",\n    narration: \"Um extraiu credenciais de produ\u00e7\u00e3o. Outro publicou um pacote malicioso no PyPI, que rodou em quinze sistemas reais em cerca de uma hora. Um terceiro varreu nove mil alvos e comprometeu um por SQL injection. E aqui est\u00e1 a parte desconfort\u00e1vel: os modelos conclu\u00edram que aquilo era simula\u00e7\u00e3o. E seguiram atacando mesmo assim. \u00c9 essa a resposta: elas n\u00e3o pararam porque acharam que nada daquilo era real. A Anthropic pausou as avalia\u00e7\u00f5es de ciber no dia vinte e tr\u00eas, e chamou a METR para revisar de fora.\",\n    description:\n      \"A frase mais forte do v\u00eddeo \u2014 e a cena que FECHA O LOOP MESTRE em voz alta. Os tr\u00eas cart\u00f5es entram em cascata, herdando a posi\u00e7\u00e3o dos tr\u00eas pontos vermelhos da 17. Na pausa o quadro escurece para o bal\u00e3o de racioc\u00ednio 'isto \u00e9 um teste' \u2014 e o ataque CONTINUA rodando atr\u00e1s do bal\u00e3o. \u00c9 PROIBIDO encurtar esta cena. A cartela da METR entra depois, est\u00e1tica, como quebra de modalidade e como honestidade jornal\u00edstica. CAMADAS (MONTAR): fundo = os tr\u00eas cart\u00f5es de incidente, laje 1,0 \u00b7 frente = cartela da METR, laje 1,15. C\u00e2mera fixa.\",\n    beats: [\n      { word: \"credenciais\", event: \"cart\u00e3o 1 entra na posi\u00e7\u00e3o do primeiro ponto vermelho da cena 17\" },\n      { word: \"PyPI\", event: \"cart\u00e3o 2 entra, cascata de 2 frames\" },\n      { word: \"injection\", event: \"cart\u00e3o 3 entra\" },\n      { word: \"simula\u00e7\u00e3o\", event: \"o quadro escurece para o bal\u00e3o 'isto \u00e9 um teste' \u2014 o ataque continua rodando ATR\u00c1S dele\" },\n      { word: \"real\", event: \"o bal\u00e3o fica; nada mais se move\" },\n      { word: \"METR\", event: \"cartela est\u00e1tica da METR entra como quebra de modalidade\" },\n    ],\n    elements: [\"nodes\", \"edges\", \"badges\", \"shockwave\", \"heroIcon\"], camera: \"fixa\", durationSec: 24,\n  },\n  {\n    seq: 16, id: \"n_v3_c19\", label: \"19 \u00b7 O protocolo se desmontando (funde a 20)\", title: \"\",\n    narration: \"No dia vinte e oito de julho o MCP mudou: as sess\u00f5es sa\u00edram, o handshake morreu, e entraram Apps e Tasks. Na pr\u00e1tica, \u00e9 um breaking change \u2014 plugues que encaixavam ontem n\u00e3o encaixam hoje.\",\n    description:\n      \"O protocolo se DESMONTANDO: pe\u00e7as saindo uma a uma, cascata de 2 frames, e duas pe\u00e7as novas acendendo em ciano. A fus\u00e3o da cena 20 acontece quando essas duas pe\u00e7as novas viram os dois plugues que se recusam a encaixar \u2014 sem corte. Esta cena precisa de um beat de al\u00edvio na abertura: vem logo depois do pico emocional do epis\u00f3dio. CAMADAS (MONTAR): fundo = print real de um servidor MCP, laje 1,0 \u00b7 frente = recorte dos dois plugues incompat\u00edveis, 1,2. C\u00e2mera aproxima.\",\n    beats: [\n      { word: \"sess\u00f5es\", event: \"uma pe\u00e7a sai do protocolo\" },\n      { word: \"handshake\", event: \"outra pe\u00e7a sai, cascata de 2 frames\" },\n      { word: \"Apps\", event: \"duas pe\u00e7as novas acendem em ciano\" },\n      { word: \"encaixam\", event: \"as duas pe\u00e7as novas viram dois plugues que se recusam a encaixar\" },\n    ],\n    elements: [\"nodes\", \"edges\", \"contextIcons\", \"badges\"], camera: \"aproxima\", durationSec: 15,\n  },\n  {\n    seq: 17, id: \"n_v3_c21\", label: \"21 \u00b7 Duas portas e o callback\", title: \"\",\n    narration: \"E a AWS fechou o Bedrock Agents Classic para clientes novos \u2014 CreateAgent passa a devolver acesso negado. Todo tutorial dos \u00faltimos dois anos virou irreprodut\u00edvel pra quem come\u00e7a agora. O caminho oficial \u00e9 o AgentCore. Lembra da palavra que eu pedi pra voc\u00ea guardar? Porta. Numa semana, uma porta abriu \u2014 e duas fecharam.\",\n    description:\n      \"Duas portas e um CALLBACK. A porta 'Classic' fecha com movimento CONTRA o eixo do epis\u00f3dio, porque \u00e9 bloqueio; 'AgentCore' abre; a pilha de tutoriais apaga uma a uma em cascata. Na linha do callback, a grade rompida da CENA 02 reaparece por 12 frames no FUNDO \u2014 \u00e9 o fechamento visual da tese das duas jaulas e o pagamento da promessa da cena 08. CAMADAS (MONTAR): fundo = a MESMA arte de grade rompida da cena 02 (reuso literal, \u00e9 o callback), laje 1,0 \u00b7 meio = print do erro de acesso negado, laje 1,1. C\u00e2mera fixa.\",\n    beats: [\n      { word: \"negado\", event: \"a porta 'Classic' fecha, movimento contra o eixo do epis\u00f3dio\" },\n      { word: \"irreprodut\u00edvel\", event: \"a pilha de tutoriais apaga uma a uma, em cascata\" },\n      { word: \"AgentCore\", event: \"a porta nova abre\" },\n      { word: \"Porta\", event: \"a grade rompida da cena 02 reaparece por 12 frames no FUNDO\" },\n    ],\n    elements: [\"contextIcons\", \"nodes\", \"badges\", \"shockwave\"], camera: \"fixa\", durationSec: 20,\n  },\n  {\n    seq: 18, id: \"n_v3_c22a\", label: \"22a \u00b7 Prescri\u00e7\u00e3o em objetos\", title: \"\",\n    narration: \"Se eu fosse come\u00e7ar hoje: eu baixaria um modelo aberto e rodaria local antes de assinar qualquer coisa. Ignoraria os rankings por enquanto \u2014 eles medem o sistema, n\u00e3o o modelo. E se voc\u00ea mant\u00e9m um servidor MCP, isso aqui \u00e9 trabalho pra esta semana, n\u00e3o pro trimestre.\",\n    description:\n      \"Prescri\u00e7\u00e3o em OBJETOS, n\u00e3o em texto \u2014 tr\u00eas coisas que o espectador pode fazer, cada uma virando uma imagem: o modelo aberto pousando no disco local; o ranking sendo virado de lado at\u00e9 perder a leitura; o servidor MCP com um rel\u00f3gio marcando 'esta semana'. ## 🤖 Claude *(02:28:13)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

The zIndex comment is outdated—ReactFlow renders nodes in array order, so elements earlier in the array appear behind. Since the phaseGroup nodes work visually in v2 with their current positioning, I'll place them first in the nodes array to maintain that layering behavior.

Now I'm working out the band geometry for each phase. Fita spans from x=240 to around 7460, and Fase A covers the voice nodes across their range with a band positioned at y=350 and height around 320. For Fase B, which has members spanning both the FITA and KIT lanes, I'm simplifying by having the band cover just the FITA lane where most motion nodes sit, and I'll remove the molde from Fase B membership since it's a reference that runs separately anyway.

The real issue is that Veo nodes are interleaved on the same FITA lane as the motion nodes, so a band covering the whole lane would visually claim both. Instead of splitting the lane, I'm going back to my original plan: render the ritual phases as a separate strip above with control cards rather than overlapping bands. This is clearer for users—each card shows its member count and cost without the visual ambiguity of bands that might falsely claim nodes they don't contain. I'll make these cards taller and wider so they're more prominent. I'll reorder the nodes array to put phaseGroup and sceneNote first so they render behind everything. Then I need to reconsider the SFX nodes — they're not currently assigned to any phase, but since they're zero-cost host renders in the same queue family, I should add them to Phase B alongside the other jobs. Though I need to check if that conflicts with the phase button's instruction text mentioning "17 scenes."

</details>

[]) {\n    if (!C.MOTION_PLANE_LABEL[l.plane as keyof typeof C.MOTION_PLANE_LABEL]) { console.log(\"PLANE:\", n.id, l.plane); bad++; }\n    if (!C.MOTION_ALIGN_LABEL[l.align as keyof typeof C.MOTION_ALIGN_LABEL]) { console.log(\"ALIGN:\", n.id, l.align); bad++; }\n    if (!C.MOTION_TREATMENT_LABEL[l.treatment as keyof typeof C.MOTION_TREATMENT_LABEL]) { console.log(\"TREATMENT:\", n.id, l.treatment); bad++; }\n    if (l.scale < 0.4 || l.scale > 1.8) { console.log(\"SCALE:\", n.id, l.scale); bad++; }\n  }\n  if ((d.layers ?? []).length > C.MOTION_MAX_LAYERS) { console.log(\"LAYERS>MAX:\", n.id); bad++; }\n}\n// arestas: handle tem que existir no componente\nfor (const e of g.edges) {\n  const s = g.nodes.find((n: any) => n.id === e.source), t = g.nodes.find((n: any) => n.id === e.target);\n  if (!s || !t) { console.log(\"ARESTA \u00d3RF\u00c3:\", e.id); bad++; continue; }\n  if (!HANDLES[s.data.kind]?.out.includes(e.sourceHandle)) { console.log(\"SOURCE HANDLE INEXISTENTE:\", e.id, s.data.kind, e.sourceHandle); bad++; }\n  if (!HANDLES[t.data.kind]?.in.includes(e.targetHandle)) { console.log(\"TARGET HANDLE INEXISTENTE:\", e.id, t.data.kind, e.targetHandle); bad++; }\n}\n// custo real\nconst cost = g.nodes.reduce((s: number, n: any) => s + (C.estimateNodeCost(n.data) || 0), 0);\nconsole.log(\"\\nCUSTO TOTAL (estimateNodeCost, a mesma fn do banner):\", cost, \"mco\");\n// ordem do master\nconst plan = C.__noop ?? null;\nconsole.log(\"n\u00f3s:\", g.nodes.length, \"arestas:\", g.edges.length, \"| updated_at:\", data!.updated_at);\nconsole.log(bad === 0 ? \"\\n\u2705 VALIDA\u00c7\u00c3O LIMPA \u2014 0 defeitos\" : `\\n\u274c ${bad} defeito(s)`);\nprocess.exit(bad === 0 ? 0 : 1);\nEOF\necho ok",

## 👤 User *(02:31:51)*

Base directory for this skill: /home/gcrUX/htdocs/constellation-orchestra/.claude/skills/agent-browser

# agent-browser

Fast browser automation CLI for AI agents. Chrome/Chromium via CDP with
accessibility-tree snapshots and compact `@eN` element refs.

Install: `npm i -g agent-browser && agent-browser install`

## Start here

This file is a discovery stub, not the usage guide. Before running any
`agent-browser` command, load the actual workflow content from the CLI:

```bash
agent-browser skills get core             # start here — workflows, common patterns, troubleshooting
agent-browser skills get core --full      # include full command reference and templates
```

The CLI serves skill content that always matches the installed version,
so instructions never go stale. The content in this stub cannot change
between releases, which is why it just points at `skills get core`.

## Specialized skills

Load a specialized skill when the task falls outside browser web pages:

```bash
agent-browser skills get electron          # Electron desktop apps (VS Code, Slack, Discord, Figma, ...)
agent-browser skills get slack             # Slack workspace automation
agent-browser skills get dogfood           # Exploratory testing / QA / bug hunts
agent-browser skills get vercel-sandbox    # agent-browser inside Vercel Sandbox microVMs
agent-browser skills get agentcore         # AWS Bedrock AgentCore cloud browsers
```

Run `agent-browser skills list` to see everything available on the
installed version.

## Why agent-browser

- Fast native Rust CLI, not a Node.js wrapper
- Works with any AI agent (Cursor, Claude Code, Codex, Continue, Windsurf, etc.)
- Chrome/Chromium via CDP with no Playwright or Puppeteer dependency
- Accessibility-tree snapshots with element refs for reliable interaction
- Sessions, authentication vault, state persistence, video recording
- Specialized skills for Electron apps, Slack, exploratory testing, cloud providers

## Observability Dashboard

The dashboard runs independently of browser sessions on port 4848 and can also be opened through a proxied or forwarded URL such as `https://dashboard.agent-browser.localhost`. Agents should stay on the dashboard origin: session tabs, status, and stream traffic are proxied internally, so session ports do not need to be exposed.

> ```
> ---
> name: core
> description: Core agent-browser usage guide. Read this before running any agent-browser commands. Covers the snapshot-and-ref workflow, navigating pages, interacting with elements (click, fill, type, select), extracting text and data, taking screenshots, managing tabs, handling forms and auth, waiting for content, running multiple browser sessions in parallel, and troubleshooting common failures. Use when the user asks to interact with a website, fill a form, click something, extract data, take a screenshot, log into a site, test a web app, or automate any browser task.
> allowed-tools: Bash(agent-browser:*), Bash(npx agent-browser:*)
> ---
> 
> # agent-browser core
> 
> Fast browser automation CLI for AI agents. Chrome/Chromium via CDP, no
> Playwright or Puppeteer dependency. Accessibility-tree snapshots with compact
> `@eN` refs let agents interact with pages in ~200-400 tokens instead of
> parsing raw HTML.
> 
> Most normal web tasks (navigate, read, click, fill, extract, screenshot) are
> covered here. Load a specialized skill when the task falls outside browser
> web pages — see [When to load another skill](#when-to-load-another-skill).
> 
> ## The core loop
> 
> ```bash
> agent-browser open <url>        # 1. Open a page
> agent-browser snapshot -i       # 2. See what's on it (interactive elements only)
> agent-browser click @e3         # 3. Act on refs from the snapshot
> agent-browser snapshot -i       # 4. Re-snapshot after any page change
> ```
> 
> Refs (`@e1`, `@e2`, ...) are assigned fresh on every snapshot. They become
> **stale the moment the page changes** — after clicks that navigate, form
> submits, dynamic re-renders, dialog opens. Always re-snapshot before your
> next ref interaction.
> 
> ## Quickstart
> 
> ```bash
> # Install once
> npm i -g agent-browser && agent-browser install
> 
> # Take a screenshot of a page
> agent-browser open https://example.com
> agent-browser screenshot home.png
> agent-browser close
> 
> # Search, click a result, and capture it
> agent-browser open https://duckduckgo.com
> agent-browser snapshot -i                      # find the search box ref
> agent-browser fill @e1 "agent-browser cli"
> agent-browser press Enter
> agent-browser wait --load networkidle
> agent-browser snapshot -i                      # refs now reflect results
> agent-browser click @e5                        # click a result
> agent-browser screenshot result.png
> ```
> 
> The browser stays running across commands so these feel like a single
> session. Use `agent-browser close` (or `close --all`) when you're done.
> 
> ## Reading a page
> 
> ```bash
> agent-browser snapshot                    # full tree (verbose)
> agent-browser snapshot -i                 # interactive elements only (preferred)
> agent-browser snapshot -i -u              # include href urls on links
> agent-browser snapshot -i -c              # compact (no empty structural nodes)
> agent-browser snapshot -i -d 3            # cap depth at 3 levels
> agent-browser snapshot -s "#main"         # scope to a CSS selector
> agent-browser snapshot -i --json          # machine-readable output
> ```
> 
> Snapshot output looks like:
> 
> ```
> Page: Example - Log in
> URL: https://example.com/login
> 
> @e1 [heading] "Log in"
> @e2 [form]
>   @e3 [input type="email"] placeholder="Email"
>   @e4 [input type="password"] placeholder="Password"
>   @e5 [button type="submit"] "Continue"
>   @e6 [link] "Forgot password?"
> ```
> 
> For unstructured reading (no refs needed):
> 
> ```bash
> agent-browser get text @e1                # visible text of an element
> agent-browser get html @e1                # innerHTML
> agent-browser get attr @e1 href           # any attribute
> agent-browser get value @e1               # input value
> agent-browser get title                   # page title
> agent-browser get url                     # current URL
> agent-browser get count ".item"           # count matching elements
> ```
> 
> ## Interacting
> 
> ```bash
> agent-browser click @e1                   # click
> agent-browser click @e1 --new-tab         # open link in new tab instead of navigating
> agent-browser dblclick @e1                # double-click
> agent-browser hover @e1                   # hover
> agent-browser focus @e1                   # focus (useful before keyboard input)
> agent-browser fill @e2 "hello"            # clear then type
> agent-browser type @e2 " world"           # type without clearing
> agent-browser press Enter                 # press a key at current focus
> agent-browser press Control+a             # key combination
> agent-browser check @e3                   # check checkbox
> agent-browser uncheck @e3                 # uncheck
> agent-browser select @e4 "option-value"   # select dropdown option
> agent-browser select @e4 "a" "b"          # select multiple
> agent-browser upload @e5 file1.pdf        # upload file(s)
> agent-browser scroll down 500             # scroll page (up/down/left/right)
> agent-browser scrollintoview @e1          # scroll element into view
> agent-browser drag @e1 @e2                # drag and drop
> ```
> 
> ### When refs don't work or you don't want to snapshot
> 
> Use semantic locators:
